Hello, Dr. Lee. Thank you so much for taking the time to see me today. I really appreciate it. 

I, well... I hope I'm not wasting your time with this. It's probably just my age—I'm sure it's nothing terrible—but my head has been feeling just a little bit foggy lately. Oh, well... I wouldn't say I've been terribly sad or anything like that. I don't want to sound ungrateful, you know? I have a lovely life. But... I suppose I have been a little less enthusiastic lately.

Oh, my, no... I don't think so. I mean, everyone forgets a word here and there at my age, don't they? Just a little tip-of-the-tongue thing, nothing serious.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, goodness... well, sleep has been fine, I suppose. Just the usual waking up a bit early, which I think is quite normal for someone my age, isn't it? 

As for my appetite... well, I don't want to sound like I'm complaining or not taking care of myself. I know how important it is to eat properly. But... I suppose I haven't been terribly hungry lately. It's just that cooking for one can be such a bother, especially with the kitchen all dusty from the work they're doing. 

Most days, I just sort of... pick at a piece of toast or maybe have a little bowl of soup. Food just hasn't sounded very appealing to me, if I'm being perfectly honest. And I did notice the other day that a few of my skirts are feeling a little loose around the waist. I haven't been trying to lose weight or anything silly like that, I promise! I just haven't had much of an appetite. 

I hope you don't think I'm neglecting my health. I really am trying to eat when I can. Is it okay that my appetite has been a little off? I'm sure it will bounce right back once the house is put back together.

Oh, dear... no, no, I wouldn't use words like that. Hopeless or worthless? Goodness, that sounds so... so dramatic, doesn't it?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well... I suppose that's actually the main reason I came to see you today, if I'm being perfectly honest. I didn't want to make a fuss, but... well, my head just feels so terribly foggy lately. 

It seems to be like this most days, regardless of how I'm feeling. I just keep mixing up the simplest things. Like... oh, getting my appointment times confused, or forgetting the dates for the new exhibits at the museum where I help out. The other day, I even forgot which volunteer I had already called on the phone! I felt so dreadful about it, having to apologize for calling them twice. 

I know getting older means we forget a name here or there, and I don't want to sound like I'm complaining... but this just feels a little different than usual. It's quite embarrassing, really, to be so scatterbrained. I do hope you don't think I'm just being careless or not paying attention. I try very hard to keep everything organized. Is it normal for things to get this foggy? I'm hoping it's just a passing phase and I haven't wasted your time bringing it up...

Oh, well... I suppose I have been a little bit, yes. I know at my age, you can't expect to have the energy of a young person, and I really don't want to sound like I'm just complaining about getting older...

I mean... of course, having the house torn up makes a person just a tiny bit tense, just wondering when the plumbers will show up and trying to keep the dust out of the living room. But... no, I'm not sitting around worrying about things, and I certainly don't feel restless. \n\nI've always been a pretty calm person, really. I just try to take things as they come, you know? Is it alright that I don't have that symptom for you?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble finding the right words, getting lost or confused in places you know well, or difficulty following steps in tasks you've done many times before, even on days when you're feeling relatively okay otherwise?\n\nPatient: Oh, my, no... Oh, well... let me think about that for a moment. I don't want to give you the wrong answer. 

I suppose... well, it all sort of crept up on me together, really. The fogginess and the... the feeling so low and slowed down. I've been feeling this way, the low part, for about three months now, I'd say. And the memory trouble seemed to start right around the same time.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well... oh, I suppose... I don't want to sound like a complainer, really. But yes, if I'm being perfectly honest, I have felt a bit... down. Just sort of flat and empty, I suppose you could call it. 

It's not that I'm crying all day or anything dramatic like that! I wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself. I still get up and get dressed and try to keep a smile on my face for everyone. But underneath it all... yes, it's just been this very quiet, heavy sort of sadness, nearly every day for these past few months. 

I've tried so hard to just shake it off, you know? I keep telling myself I have so much to be thankful for and I shouldn't be feeling this way. I hope you don't think poorly of me for not being able to just snap out of it. Is it normal to feel that kind of flatness at my age? I do hope I'm giving you the right kind of information... I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly.

It's just that cooking for one can be such a bother, especially with the kitchen all dusty from the work they're doing. \n\nMost days, I just sort of... pick at a piece of toast or maybe have a little bowl of soup. Food just hasn't sounded very appealing to me, if I'm being perfectly honest. And I did notice the other day that a few of my skirts are feeling a little loose around the waist. I haven't been trying to lose weight or anything silly like that, I promise! I just haven't had much of an appetite. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm neglecting my health. I really am trying to eat when I can. Is it okay that my appetite has been a little off? I'm sure it will bounce right back once the house is put back together.\n\nDoctor: Over these past four months, have you found yourself feeling unusually guilty, worthless, or hopeless about things, even things that wouldn't normally bother you?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... no, no, I wouldn't use words like that. Hopeless or worthless? Goodness, that sounds so... so dramatic, doesn't it? \n\nI mean, I suppose everyone has days where they feel they could have done something better, or they feel a little silly about a mistake. Like the other day, I dropped a whole carton of eggs on the floor. I did feel terrible about the mess, especially since the kitchen is already in such a state. I felt quite foolish for being so clumsy. \n\nBut... no, I certainly don't feel worthless. I try to stay positive, I really do. I know I have a lot to be thankful for, and I wouldn't want to sound ungrateful or like I'm feeling sorry for myself. I try very hard to look on the bright side of things. \n\nI hope it's alright that I don't feel those things you mentioned. I know you're just doing your job asking, but I promise I'm trying my best to keep a good attitude about everything. Is that the sort of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Over these past four months, have you ever had thoughts that life isn't worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Oh, heavens, no! Oh my... no, no, I would never do anything like that. \n\nI mean, I know life can be a little difficult sometimes, but... well, I would never even entertain a thought like that. I think it would be terribly ungrateful to... to not want to be here. I have my little routines, and even with the kitchen mess and being a bit tired, I still have so much to be thankful for. I would never want to do something so... so drastic, or cause that kind of trouble for anyone. \n\nI hope you know I'm trying my very best to take care of myself. You don't think I seem like the type of person who would do that, do you? I know it's your job to ask everyone these things, and I don't mind at all, but I promise you I am perfectly safe. Oh, dear... well, I suppose other people have noticed a little bit. I really didn't want it to come to that, I try so hard to keep everything running smoothly so no one has to worry about me.

I hope I'm explaining things clearly enough for you.\n\nDoctor: Over these past four months, have you noticed feeling persistently sad, down, or losing interest or pleasure in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I wouldn't say I've been terribly sad or anything like that. I don't want to sound ungrateful, you know? I have a lovely life. But... I suppose I have been a little less enthusiastic lately. \n\nIt's silly, really. I usually just adore my watercolors, and I'm always out tending to my tomato plants... and I used to go line dancing with the girls, and I'd sit by the window just to watch the cardinals out back. But lately, they all just sort of... sit there. I look at my paints or the garden, and I just can't seem to make myself care about them the way I used to. \n\nI'm sure it's just the stress of the remodel wearing me out, though. It's perfectly normal to need a little break from hobbies, isn't it? I hope I'm not making a fuss over nothing.\n\nDoctor: Over these past four months, have you found yourself worrying a lot more than usual, or feeling unusually tense, restless, or on edge, even about small things?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean... of course, having the house torn up makes a person just a tiny bit tense, just wondering when the plumbers will show up and trying to keep the dust out of the living room. But... no, I'm not sitting around worrying about things, and I certainly don't feel restless. \n\nI've always been a pretty calm person, really. I just try to take things as they come, you know? I hope I'm not being unhelpful by saying no to that one. I just want to be completely honest with you, and I really haven't noticed feeling on edge at all. Is it alright that I don't have that symptom for you?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble finding the right words, getting lost or confused in places you know well, or difficulty following steps in tasks you've done many times before, even on days when you're feeling relatively okay otherwise?\n\nPatient: Oh, my, no... I don't think so. I mean, everyone forgets a word here and there at my age, don't they? Just a little tip-of-the-tongue thing, nothing serious. \n\nI haven't gotten lost anywhere, goodness no. I know my way around town just fine. And I still do all my own cooking and baking without any trouble following recipes, though I suppose I haven't been doing quite as much of it lately with the kitchen being such a mess from the remodel. \n\nBut no, I wouldn't say I'm getting confused or forgetting how to do things. I'm still sharp as a tack, for an old lady! I hope that's a good thing to hear? I'd hate for you to think I was losing my marbles or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Over these past four months, how has your sleep and appetite been—have you noticed any changes in how much you're sleeping or eating compared to before?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, sleep has been fine, I suppose. Just the usual waking up a bit early, which I think is quite normal for someone my age, isn't it? \n\nAs for my appetite... well, I don't want to sound like I'm complaining or not taking care of myself. I know how important it is to eat properly. But... I suppose I haven't been terribly hungry lately. It's just that cooking for one can be such a bother, especially with the kitchen all dusty from the work they're doing. \n\nMost days, I just sort of... pick at a piece of toast or maybe have a little bowl of soup. Food just hasn't sounded very appealing to me, if I'm being perfectly honest. And I did notice the other day that a few of my skirts are feeling a little loose around the waist. I haven't been trying to lose weight or anything silly like that, I promise! I just haven't had much of an appetite. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm neglecting my health. I really am trying to eat when I can. Is it okay that my appetite has been a little off? I'm sure it will bounce right back once the house is put back together.\n\nDoctor: Over these past four months, have you found yourself feeling unusually guilty, worthless, or hopeless about things, even things that wouldn't normally bother you?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... no, no, I wouldn't use words like that. Hopeless or worthless? Goodness, that sounds so... so dramatic, doesn't it? \n\nI mean, I suppose everyone has days where they feel they could have done something better, or they feel a little silly about a mistake. Like the other day, I dropped a whole carton of eggs on the floor. I did feel terrible about the mess, especially since the kitchen is already in such a state. I felt quite foolish for being so clumsy. \n\nBut... no, I certainly don't feel worthless. I try to stay positive, I really do. I know I have a lot to be thankful for, and I wouldn't want to sound ungrateful or like I'm feeling sorry for myself. I try very hard to look on the bright side of things. \n\nI hope it's alright that I don't feel those things you mentioned. I know you're just doing your job asking, but I promise I'm trying my best to keep a good attitude about everything. Is that the sort of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Over these past four months, have you ever had thoughts that life isn't worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Oh, heavens, no! Oh my... no, no, I would never do anything like that. \n\nI mean, I know life can be a little difficult sometimes, but... well, I would never even entertain a thought like that. I think it would be terribly ungrateful to... to not want to be here. I have my little routines, and even with the kitchen mess and being a bit tired, I still have so much to be thankful for. I would never want to do something so... so drastic, or cause that kind of trouble for anyone. \n\nI hope you know I'm trying my very best to take care of myself. You don't think I seem like the type of person who would do that, do you? I know it's your job to ask everyone these things, and I don't mind at all, but I promise you I am perfectly safe. I hope that puts your mind at ease?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your memory and mental fogginess are about the same every day, or do they seem better on days when you're feeling a bit more like your usual self emotionally?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose that's actually the main reason I came to see you today, if I'm being perfectly honest. I didn't want to make a fuss, but... well, my head just feels so terribly foggy lately. \n\nIt seems to be like this most days, regardless of how I'm feeling. I just keep mixing up the simplest things. Like... oh, getting my appointment times confused, or forgetting the dates for the new exhibits at the museum where I help out. The other day, I even forgot which volunteer I had already called on the phone! I felt so dreadful about it, having to apologize for calling them twice. \n\nI know getting older means we forget a name here or there, and I don't want to sound like I'm complaining... but this just feels a little different than usual. It's quite embarrassing, really, to be so scatterbrained. I do hope you don't think I'm just being careless or not paying attention. I try very hard to keep everything organized. Is it normal for things to get this foggy? I'm hoping it's just a passing phase and I haven't wasted your time bringing it up...\n\nDoctor: Over these past four months, have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy during the day, even when you haven't been especially active?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I have been a little bit, yes. I know at my age, you can't expect to have the energy of a young person, and I really don't want to sound like I'm just complaining about getting older...\n\nBut... well, yes, it does seem like I'm awfully drained lately. Even when I haven't done anything strenuous at all. I'll wake up in the morning, and I just still feel so tired, even after sleeping. And then... oh, it's a bit embarrassing to admit, but just doing the simple things, like washing up and making a little bit of breakfast... by the time I'm finished with just that, I feel like I need to go and sit down again. \n\nI feel so terribly lazy saying that out loud. I promise I try very hard to keep active and not just sit around the house all day. I hope you don't think I'm just letting myself go or making excuses. Do you think it's alright that I'm feeling that worn out, or am I just making a fuss over nothing?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed whether your memory and concentration difficulties are worse at certain times of the day, such as mornings versus evenings, or do they stay pretty much the same throughout the day?\n\nPatient: Well... as I was mentioning a moment ago, that foggy feeling really is the main reason I made the appointment to see you. It's just so terribly frustrating to keep mixing up those simple little things, like the museum exhibit dates or... or which of the volunteers I've already spoken to on the phone. \n\nAs for the time of day... oh, I suppose it stays pretty much the same from morning to night, if I'm being honest. It just sort of... lingers, I'm afraid. Whether it's breakfast time or after supper, I still find myself having to double-check things like my appointment times because my head just feels so thick all day long. \n\nI do hope I'm explaining that clearly enough for you? I really try my hardest to stay sharp and push through it, and I write everything down... I don't want you to think I'm just letting my mind go or not making an effort. Is it alright that it doesn't seem to change much depending on the hour? I hope that's a helpful answer.\n\nDoctor: Thinking back, did this foggy, forgetful feeling start around the same time you began feeling less interested in your hobbies and more tired, or did the memory issues actually begin before those other changes?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... let me think about that for a moment. I don't want to give you the wrong answer. \n\nI suppose... well, it all sort of crept up on me together, really. The fogginess and the... the feeling so low and slowed down. I've been feeling this way, the low part, for about three months now, I'd say. And the memory trouble seemed to start right around the same time. \n\nI really didn't want to complain about it earlier, because I know everyone gets a bit down in the dumps sometimes, especially at my age. I tried to just put on a brave face and keep going with my museum work and everything else. I hope you don't think I was trying to hide it from you... I just didn't want to seem like a burden or like I was making a mountain out of a molehill. \n\nDoes that make sense, that they would start around the same time? I hope that's the kind of information you were looking for... I'm really trying my best to remember it all accurately for you.\n\nDoctor: When someone teaches you something new—like a new volunteer's name or a new procedure at the museum—do you find you have trouble learning and remembering it afterward, or is it more that you have trouble concentrating enough to take it in in the first place?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose it's a bit of both, really. I hope you don't mind me repeating myself, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answer. \n\nWhen someone tells me a new name or explains something, I try so, so hard to pay attention. I really do. I don't want them to think I'm being rude or not listening to them. But... well, like I was saying earlier, that awful foggy feeling in my head just gets in the way. It's like I can't quite take it in, and then I can't hold onto it afterward, either. \n\nThat really is what brought me in to see you in the first place. I just felt so embarrassed because I keep mixing up those simple things—the appointment times, the exhibit dates, trying to remember which volunteer I've already called and which one I haven't. It all just gets so jumbled, no matter how hard I concentrate. \n\nI feel so silly admitting all of this, I really do. I hope I'm not sounding too foolish? Does that answer your question alright? I'm trying my very best to explain how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few months, would you say you've felt down, low, or emotionally flat for most of the day, nearly every day—even apart from your energy and interest in hobbies?\n\nPatient: Well... oh, I suppose... I don't want to sound like a complainer, really. But yes, if I'm being perfectly honest, I have felt a bit... down. Just sort of flat and empty, I suppose you could call it. \n\nIt's not that I'm crying all day or anything dramatic like that! I wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself. I still get up and get dressed and try to keep a smile on my face for everyone. But underneath it all... yes, it's just been this very quiet, heavy sort of sadness, nearly every day for these past few months. \n\nI've tried so hard to just shake it off, you know? I keep telling myself I have so much to be thankful for and I shouldn't be feeling this way. I hope you don't think poorly of me for not being able to just snap out of it. Is it normal to feel that kind of flatness at my age? I do hope I'm giving you the right kind of information... I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Have any family members, friends, or coworkers at the museum mentioned noticing you seem more forgetful or confused, or is this something you mainly notice yourself?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well, I suppose other people have noticed a little bit. I really didn't want it to come to that, I try so hard to keep everything running smoothly so no one has to worry about me. \n\nBut, yes... my husband, bless his heart, he's had to step in quite a bit lately. I've—well, I've missed a few of my own doctor visits, which is so unlike me. And I got the senior center schedule all tangled up... I felt just terrible about it. I didn't want to cause any trouble for anyone. \n\nSo now he's been helping me with the phone calls and keeping track of the calendars, and even making some of the decisions around the house, like when we needed a repairman. I used to handle all of that myself, and I really prided myself on being capable. I hope you don't think I'm just being lazy and letting him do all the work. I really am trying my best to keep up, but I just... I get so turned around. \n\nIs it alright that I'm telling you this? I just feel so ashamed that I can't manage my own schedule right now. I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time with all this...\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [doctor] ==========
{"candidates": ["F32.9"], "note": "Depressive symptoms (anhedonia, low mood, fatigue, appetite/weight loss, cognitive fogginess) are pervasive, present nearly every day for ~3-4 months, with functional decline confirmed by husband's observations. No anxiety symptoms reported, ruling out R41.3 as primary complaint; cognitive symptoms appear consistent with pseudodementia from depression rather than an independent neurocognitive disorder, and no true anterograde amnesia, word-finding deficits, or getting lost noted. Symptom pattern strongly supports major depressive episode with prominent cognitive complaints.", "is_final": true}

I really am trying my best to keep up, but I just... I get so turned around. \n\nIs it alright that I'm telling you this? I just feel so ashamed that I can't manage my own schedule right now.
